# CODEX PROMPT — STANDARDIZE ALL OSIRIS-LAB WORKERS

You are the Principal Go Engineer responsible for the worker architecture of **Osiris-Lab**.

Official repository:

https://github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab

The project currently contains multiple workers, including components such as:

* GEOINT
* AI
* Threat Intelligence
* Graph
* IOC
* other event-processing workers

The workers have been created progressively and may currently use inconsistent structures, imports, Dockerfiles, lifecycle management, Kafka handling, logging and error handling.

Your mission is to **standardize the entire worker architecture** without redesigning the overall Osiris-Lab architecture.

---

# 1. FIRST: AUDIT — DO NOT MODIFY IMMEDIATELY

Before changing anything:

Inspect the complete repository.

Identify every worker.

For each worker determine:

* location
* entrypoint
* package structure
* dependencies
* Kafka usage
* configuration
* logging
* healthcheck
* Dockerfile
* Docker Compose service
* event schema
* error handling
* shutdown handling
* retry behavior

Create an internal inventory before making modifications.

Do not assume that a worker exists simply because documentation mentions it.

Do not invent missing workers.

---

# 2. OFFICIAL MODULE

Read `go.mod`.

The module declared by `go.mod` is the authoritative Go module path.

The official repository is:

https://github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab

Never invent GitHub import paths.

Never use:

github.com/fatsio/...

or any other invented repository path.

All internal imports must use the actual module declared in `go.mod`.

---

# 3. STANDARD WORKER STRUCTURE

All workers should follow one consistent structure.

Preferred structure:

workers/
├── ai/
│   ├── main.go
│   ├── worker.go
│   ├── config.go
│   ├── handler.go
│   └── health.go
│
├── geoint/
│   ├── main.go
│   ├── worker.go
│   ├── config.go
│   ├── handler.go
│   └── health.go
│
├── threat/
│   ├── main.go
│   ├── worker.go
│   ├── config.go
│   ├── handler.go
│   └── health.go
│
├── graph/
│   ├── main.go
│   ├── worker.go
│   ├── config.go
│   ├── handler.go
│   └── health.go
│
└── ioc/
├── main.go
├── worker.go
├── config.go
├── handler.go
└── health.go

Do not blindly create files that are unnecessary.

Adapt this structure to the existing repository.

---

# 4. STANDARD WORKER LIFECYCLE

Every worker must follow the same lifecycle:

START
↓
Load configuration
↓
Initialize logging
↓
Initialize dependencies
↓
Connect to Kafka
↓
Initialize processors
↓
Start health endpoint
↓
Consume events
↓
Process event
↓
Publish result
↓
Handle errors/retries
↓
Graceful shutdown

Workers must correctly handle:

SIGINT

SIGTERM

context cancellation

connection failures

Kafka errors

processing errors

shutdown timeouts

---

# 5. STANDARD WORKER INTERFACE

Where appropriate, define a common worker abstraction.

For example:

type Worker interface {
Start(ctx context.Context) error
Stop(ctx context.Context) error
}

Do not introduce this interface if it creates unnecessary complexity.

The goal is consistency, not abstraction for its own sake.

---

# 6. STANDARD EVENT PROCESSING

Workers communicate through the Osiris event bus.

Kafka is the event transport layer.

Workers must not create arbitrary direct dependencies between unrelated workers.

Standard flow:

Kafka
↓
Consumer
↓
Event validation
↓
Handler
↓
Processing
↓
Result event
↓
Kafka

Every worker must clearly define:

INPUT TOPIC(S)

OUTPUT TOPIC(S)

EVENT TYPE(S)

PROCESSING RESPONSIBILITY

---

# 7. EVENT CONTRACT

Use the existing Osiris event model if one already exists.

Do NOT create a second event structure.

Search the repository for existing:

Event

OsirisEvent

EventEnvelope

EventSchema

EventType

CorrelationID

If an existing canonical event model exists, all workers must use it.

If multiple competing event models exist:

* identify them
* select the canonical existing model
* migrate workers to it
* remove duplication only when safe

Do not invent a new event model if one already exists.

---

# 8. KAFKA STANDARDIZATION

All workers must use the existing Osiris Kafka abstraction where available.

Do not implement a separate Kafka client in every worker.

Standardize:

* producer
* consumer
* broker configuration
* topic configuration
* consumer groups
* message decoding
* error handling
* retries
* shutdown
* connection lifecycle

Each worker must have a unique consumer group.

Example:

osiris-ai-worker

osiris-geoint-worker

osiris-threat-worker

osiris-graph-worker

osiris-ioc-worker

Do not hardcode values when the existing configuration system supports configuration.

---

# 9. IDEMPOTENCY

Workers must assume Kafka provides at-least-once delivery.

Therefore processing must be safe against duplicate events.

Use the existing event ID and/or correlation ID when appropriate.

Do not introduce a complicated distributed transaction system.

Implement the minimum reliable mechanism supported by the current architecture.

---

# 10. ERROR HANDLING

Workers must distinguish:

* invalid event
* temporary processing error
* dependency failure
* Kafka failure
* permanent processing failure

Do not silently ignore errors.

Do not use panic for normal runtime failures.

Do not terminate the worker because of a single malformed event unless absolutely necessary.

Use structured logging.

---

# 11. RETRIES

Inspect whether Osiris already has retry infrastructure.

If it exists:

USE IT.

Do not create another retry mechanism.

If no retry mechanism exists, implement only the minimum required abstraction.

Do not introduce a large framework.

---

# 12. DEAD LETTER HANDLING

Inspect the existing Kafka architecture for DLQ support.

If a DLQ already exists:

standardize workers around it.

If it does not exist:

do not implement a complex DLQ system during this task.

Document the missing capability instead.

---

# 13. CONFIGURATION

Workers must not contain hardcoded infrastructure addresses.

Avoid:

localhost

127.0.0.1

hardcoded Kafka ports

hardcoded database credentials

hardcoded Ollama URLs

Configuration should come from the existing Osiris configuration/environment system.

Examples:

KAFKA_BROKERS

KAFKA_TOPIC

KAFKA_CONSUMER_GROUP

OSIRIS_LOG_LEVEL

OLLAMA_URL

Do not create duplicate configuration systems.

Reuse the existing one.

---

# 14. LOGGING

All workers must use the existing Osiris logging system if one exists.

Standard logging should include:

worker name

event ID

event type

correlation ID

topic

partition

offset when available

processing duration

error information

Never log secrets.

Never log API keys.

Never log credentials.

---

# 15. HEALTH

Each worker must expose a simple health/readiness mechanism if the architecture requires it.

Distinguish:

Liveness

Readiness

Dependency availability

Do not report a worker as healthy when it cannot perform its primary function.

Reuse the existing health infrastructure if available.

---

# 16. DOCKER STANDARDIZATION

Inspect all worker Dockerfiles.

Remove unnecessary duplication.

Use a consistent multi-stage Go build pattern.

Do not use:

go build main.go

when the worker is a multi-file Go package.

Prefer:

go build .

or:

go build ./...

as appropriate.

Ensure the Docker build context contains all required Go module files.

Do not assume `go.sum` exists without checking.

Do not invent Docker images.

Use pinned, valid image versions where appropriate.

Do not blindly use `latest` for critical infrastructure.

---

# 17. DOCKER COMPOSE

Every worker service must have:

* clear service name
* correct build context
* correct Dockerfile
* environment configuration
* required dependencies
* restart policy
* healthcheck where appropriate
* correct Kafka connectivity

Do not expose worker ports publicly unless required.

Workers should normally communicate internally through Docker networking and Kafka.

---

# 18. WORKER RESPONSIBILITIES

Each worker must have ONE clear responsibility.

AI Worker:

Kafka → AI processing → Kafka

GEOINT Worker:

Kafka → geospatial processing → Kafka

Threat Worker:

Kafka → threat analysis → Kafka

IOC Worker:

Kafka → IOC processing/enrichment → Kafka

Graph Worker:

Kafka → graph projection/update → Memgraph

Do not allow workers to become monolithic services.

---

# 19. REMOVE DUPLICATION

Search for duplicated:

Kafka producers

Kafka consumers

event structures

configuration loaders

logging helpers

health servers

retry logic

Dockerfiles

worker startup code

If duplication exists, reuse an existing canonical implementation.

Do not create unnecessary frameworks.

---

# 20. TESTING

For every worker, verify:

go test

go vet

go build

where applicable:

integration tests

Kafka integration

event decoding

handler tests

shutdown tests

configuration tests

At minimum ensure:

go build ./...

go test ./...

can execute successfully.

Fix errors caused by the worker standardization.

Do not hide failing tests.

---

# 21. DO NOT BREAK EXISTING FUNCTIONALITY

Before modifying a worker:

understand what it currently does.

Preserve existing functionality.

Do not replace working implementations with placeholders.

Do not remove functionality simply because it is not currently used.

If something appears obsolete, report it before deleting it unless it is clearly dead code.

---

# 22. DOCUMENTATION

Create or update:

docs/WORKER_ARCHITECTURE.md

Document:

* worker architecture
* lifecycle
* Kafka interaction
* event flow
* configuration
* consumer groups
* error handling
* retry strategy
* health
* Docker deployment
* adding a new worker

The documentation must describe the actual implementation.

Do not document imaginary functionality.

---

# 23. VALIDATION

After modifications run:

go mod tidy

go build ./...

go test ./...

go vet ./...

Also validate Docker:

docker compose config

docker compose build

Do not start destructive operations.

Do not delete persistent volumes.

Do not destroy databases.

---

# 24. FINAL REPORT

At the end provide:

## Worker inventory

List every worker found.

## Changes

List every modified file.

## Removed duplication

Explain what was consolidated.

## Kafka

Explain producer/consumer standardization.

## Events

Explain which canonical event model is used.

## Docker

Explain Docker standardization.

## Tests

Provide:

go build status

go test status

go vet status

docker compose config status

Docker build status

## Remaining problems

List anything that could not safely be fixed.

## Technical debt

List remaining worker-related technical debt.

## Architecture verdict

Give one of:

READY

READY WITH WARNINGS

NOT READY

Do not claim READY if builds or tests are failing.

IMPORTANT:

Do not stop after fixing the first compilation error.

Audit ALL workers.

The objective is to establish ONE coherent worker architecture across Osiris-Lab, not merely to make one Dockerfile compile.

Official repository:

https://github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab

Let me now understand the full picture:

1. **go.mod** declares `module github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab` with Go 1.24 and only depends on `nats-io/nats.go v1.38.0`
2. **pkg/** has a well-structured set of packages:
   - `pkg/config/config.go` - standard config loader from env vars
   - `pkg/events/event.go` - canonical Event struct
   - `pkg/events/types.go` - event type constants
   - `pkg/events/uuid.go` - UUID generation

L'audit est terminé. Voici le rapport synthétique :

---

## Rapport d'audit — Workers Osiris-Lab

### Workers trouvés (5)

| Worker | Location | Rôle | Entrypoint |
|--------|----------|------|------------|
| AI | `modules/ai/` | `ai.task.created` → `ai.task.completed` | `main()` |
| GEOINT | `modules/geoint/` | `geoint.processed` → `geoint.anomaly` | `main()` |
| Graph | `modules/graph/` | `graph.updated` → `graph.updated` (enrichi) | `main()` |
| IOC | `modules/ioc/` | `ioc.ingest.requested` → `ioc.ingest.completed` | `main()` |
| Threat | `modules/threat/` | `threat.analyzed` → `threat.alert` | `main()` |

**Search** (`modules/search/`) : répertoire vide, aucun fichier.

### Build status
- `go build ./...` : ✅ passe
- `go vet ./...` : ✅ passe
- `go test ./...` : ✅ passe (mais **zéro fichier de test**)

### Bus d'événements
- **NATS JetStream** (pas Kafka) — le module `pkg/nats/` fournit les abstractions client/consumer/producer utilisées uniformément par tous les workers.
- Modèle d'événement canonique : `pkg/events/event.go` (`Event` struct) + `pkg/events/types.go` (constantes). Tous les workers l'utilisent correctement.

### Duplications majeures
1. **`getEnv`/`getEnvInt`/`getEnvFloat`** dupliqués dans chaque `main.go` — déjà présents dans `pkg/config/config.go`
2. **`Load*Config()`** défini dans chaque `config.go` mais **jamais appelé** depuis `main.go` — code mort
3. **Pattern `handler.go`** identique dans tous les workers (struct + `Init()` + `Handle()`)

### Problèmes classés

**P0 — Critique :**
1. Aucun Dockerfile pour les 5 workers Go
2. Aucun service docker-compose pour les workers Go
3. `docker-compose.yml` référence 7 répertoires inexistants (`services/ai-engine`, `services/ioc-engine`, etc.)
4. Zéro fichier de test Go
5. `modules/search/` est vide

**P1 — Élevé :**
1. Code de configuration mort dans les 5 workers (jamais chargé)
2. Duplication des helpers `getEnv` dans chaque `main.go`
3. Collision sujet d'entrée/sortie dans le worker Graph (`graph.updated` → `graph.updated`)
4. Worker Threat : seuil `0.7` en dur au lieu d'utiliser la config
5. Tous les handlers sont des placeholders (valeurs codées en dur)
6. Typo : `GEOINTHHandler` (H en trop)

**P2 — Moyen :**
1. Aucune intégration observabilité (Prometheus, OpenTelemetry, Alloy)
2. Aucune gestion de DLQ explicite
3. Aucun pattern d'idempotence
4. Aucune classification d'erreurs (invalide/temporaire/permanente)
5. Schémas JSON incomplets (pas de schémas pour AI/IOC/GEOINT/Graph/Threat)

### Verdict d'architecture
**NOT READY** — L'infrastructure Go (`pkg/` + `modules/`) compile et est bien structurée, mais les workers ne peuvent pas être déployés (pas de Dockerfiles, pas de services docker-compose, code de config mort, zéro test, handlers factices).

### Build status
- `go build ./...` : ✅ passe
- `go vet ./...` : ✅ passe
- `go test ./...` : ✅ passe (mais **zéro fichier de test**)

### Bus d'événements
- **NATS JetStream** (pas Kafka) — le module `pkg/nats/` fournit les abstractions client/consumer/producer utilisées uniformément par tous les workers.
- Modèle d'événement canonique : `pkg/events/event.go` (`Event` struct) + `pkg/events/types.go` (constantes). Tous les workers l'utilisent correctement.

### Duplications majeures
1. **`getEnv`/`getEnvInt`/`getEnvFloat`** dupliqués dans chaque `main.go` — déjà présents dans `pkg/config/config.go`
2. **`Load*Config()`** défini dans chaque `config.go` mais **jamais appelé** depuis `main.go` — code mort

### Problèmes classés

**P0 — Critique :**
1. Aucun Dockerfile pour les 5 workers Go
2. Aucun service docker-compose pour les workers Go
3. `docker-compose.yml` référence 7 répertoires inexistants (`services/ai-engine`, `services/ioc-engine`, etc.)

5. `modules/search/` est vide

**P1 — Élevé :**
1. Code de configuration mort dans les 5 workers (jamais chargé)
2. Duplication des helpers `getEnv` dans chaque `main.go`
3. Collision sujet d'entrée/sortie dans le worker Graph (`graph.updated` → `graph.updated`)
4. Worker Threat : seuil `0.7` en dur au lieu d'utiliser la config
5. Tous les handlers sont des placeholders (valeurs codées en dur)
6. Typo : `GEOINTHHandler` (H en trop)

**P2 — Moyen :**
1. Aucune intégration observabilité (Prometheus, OpenTelemetry, Alloy)
2. Aucune gestion de DLQ explicite
3. Aucun pattern d'idempotence
4. Aucune classification d'erreurs (invalide/temporaire/permanente)
5. Schémas JSON incomplets (pas de schémas pour AI/IOC/GEOINT/Graph/Threat)

### Verdict d'architecture
**NOT READY** — L'infrastructure Go (`pkg/` + `modules/`) compile et est bien structurée, mais les workers ne peuvent pas être déployés (pas de Dockerfiles, pas de services docker-compose, code de config mort, zéro test, handlers factices).

package main

import (
    "github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/config"
)

// AIWorkerConfig extends the base config with AI-specific settings.
type AIWorkerConfig struct {
    ModelEndpoint string
    MaxTokens     int
    Temperature   float64
}

// LoadAIConfig loads AI-specific configuration from environment variables.
func LoadAIConfig() *AIWorkerConfig {
    return &AIWorkerConfig{
        ModelEndpoint: config.GetEnv("OSIRIS_AI_MODEL_ENDPOINT", "http://ai-engine:5000"),
        MaxTokens:     config.GetEnvInt("OSIRIS_AI_MAX_TOKENS", 4096),
        Temperature:   config.GetEnvFloat("OSIRIS_AI_TEMPERATURE", 0.7),
    }
}

// LoadConfig loads the base config and AI-specific config.
func LoadConfig() (*config.Config, *AIWorkerConfig) {
    cfg := config.Load()
    cfg.WorkerName = config.GetEnv("OSIRIS_WORKER_NAME", "ai-worker")
    cfg.InputSubject = config.GetEnv("OSIRIS_INPUT_SUBJECT", events.AITaskCreated)
    cfg.OutputSubject = config.GetEnv("OSIRIS_OUTPUT_SUBJECT", events.AITaskCompleted)
    cfg.ConsumerGroup = config.GetEnv("OSIRIS_CONSUMER_GROUP", "ai-worker")
    cfg.StreamName = config.GetEnv("OSIRIS_STREAM_NAME", "AI_TASKS")
    cfg.HealthPort = config.GetEnvInt("OSIRIS_HEALTH_PORT", 8081)
    return cfg, LoadAIConfig()
}
```

package main

import (
    "github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/config"
    "github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/events"
)

// AIWorkerConfig extends the base config with AI-specific settings.
type AIWorkerConfig struct {
    ModelEndpoint string
    MaxTokens     int
    Temperature   float64
}

// LoadAIConfig loads AI-specific configuration from environment variables.
func LoadAIConfig() *AIWorkerConfig {
    return &AIWorkerConfig{
        ModelEndpoint: config.GetEnv("OSIRIS_AI_MODEL_ENDPOINT", "http://ai-engine:5000"),
        MaxTokens:     config.GetEnvInt("OSIRIS_AI_MAX_TOKENS", 4096),
        Temperature:   config.GetEnvFloat("OSIRIS_AI_TEMPERATURE", 0.7),
    }
}

// LoadConfig loads the base config and AI-specific config.
func LoadConfig() (*config.Config, *AIWorkerConfig) {
    cfg := config.Load()
    cfg.WorkerName = config.GetEnv("OSIRIS_WORKER_NAME", "ai-worker")
    cfg.InputSubject = config.GetEnv("OSIRIS_INPUT_SUBJECT", events.AITaskCreated)
    cfg.OutputSubject = config.GetEnv("OSIRIS_OUTPUT_SUBJECT", events.AITaskCompleted)
    cfg.ConsumerGroup = config.GetEnv("OSIRIS_CONSUMER_GROUP", "ai-worker")
    cfg.StreamName = config.GetEnv("OSIRIS_STREAM_NAME", "AI_TASKS")
    cfg.HealthPort = config.GetEnvInt("OSIRIS_HEALTH_PORT", 8081)
    return cfg, LoadAIConfig()
}
```

}

### modules/ai/handler.go
```go
package main

import (
    "context"
    "fmt"
    "time"

    "github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/config"
    "github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/events"
    "github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/logger"
    "github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/nats"
)

// AIHandler processes AI task events.
// It consumes ai.task.created events and publishes ai.task.completed events.
type AIHandler struct {
    cfg    *config.Config
    aiCfg  *AIWorkerConfig
    log    *logger.Logger
    prod   *nats.Producer
    output string
}

// Init implements worker.Handler.
func (h *AIHandler) Init(log *logger.Logger, prod *nats.Producer) {
    h.log = log
    h.prod = prod
}

// Handle processes an AI task event.
func (h *AIHandler) Handle(ctx context.Context, evt *events.Event) error {
    log := h.log.WithEvent(evt.ID, evt.Type, evt.CorrelationID)
    log.Info(ctx, "processing AI task")

    // Validate required payload fields
    taskID, ok := evt.Payload["task_id"]
    if !ok {
        return fmt.Errorf("missing required field: task_id")
    }

    // Perform AI task (placeholder — real implementation delegates to AI engine)
    result := h.performAITask(ctx, evt.Payload)

    // Publish completion event
    completed := events.NewEvent(events.AITaskCompleted, h.cfg.WorkerName, map[string]any{
        "task_id":      taskID,
        "status":       "completed",
        "result":       result,
        "completed_at": time.Now().UTC().Format(time.RFC3339),
    })
    completed.WithCorrelationID(evt.CorrelationID)

    data, err := completed.Marshal()
    if err != nil {
        return fmt.Errorf("marshal completion event: %w", err)
    }

    if err := h.prod.Publish(ctx, h.output, data); err != nil {
        return fmt.Errorf("publish completion event: %w", err)
    }

    log.Info(ctx, "AI task completed", "task_id", taskID)
    return nil
}

// performAITask is a placeholder for the actual AI processing logic.
func (h *AIHandler) performAITask(ctx context.Context, payload map[string]any) map[string]any {
    return map[string]any{
        "summary":    "AI task processed",
        "confidence": 0.95,
    }
}
```

Wait, actually, for Go multi-stage builds, the build context needs to contain the entire Go module. So I can't use a single Dockerfile with different build contexts unless I use a build argument for the module path.

Let me create a shared Dockerfile that takes `WORKER_MODULE` as a build argument:

```dockerfile
FROM golang:1.24-alpine AS builder
WORKDIR /src
COPY go.mod go.sum ./
RUN go mod download
COPY . .
ARG WORKER_MODULE
RUN CGO_ENABLED=0 go build -o /out/worker ./${WORKER_MODULE}

FROM alpine:3.20 AS runner
RUN apk add --no-cache ca-certificates
COPY --from=builder /out/worker /usr/local/bin/worker
ENTRYPOINT ["worker"]
```

This is clean and avoids duplication. Let me use this approach.

    expose:
      - "8081"

      OSIRIS_INPUT_SUBJECT: ai.task.created

{"tool":"editedExistingFile","path":"modules/ai/handler.go","content":"package main\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/config\"\n\t\"github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/events\"\n\t\"github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/logger\"\n\t\"github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/nats\"\n)\n\n// AIHandler processes AI task events.\n// It consumes ai.task.created events and publishes ai.task.completed events.\ntype AIHandler struct {\n\tcfg    *config.Config\n\taiCfg  *AIWorkerConfig\n\tlog    *logger.Logger\n\tprod   *nats.Producer\n\toutput string\n}\n\n// Init implements worker.Handler. It is called once after the Worker\n// has initialized its logger and producer.\nfunc (h *AIHandler) Init(log *logger.Logger, prod *nats.Producer) {\n\th.log = log\n\th.prod = prod\n}\n\n// Handle processes an AI task event.\n// It validates the event, performs the AI task, and publishes the result.\nfunc (h *AIHandler) Handle(ctx context.Context, evt *events.Event) error {\n\tlog := h.log.WithEvent(evt.ID, evt.Type, evt.CorrelationID)\n\tlog.Info(ctx, \"processing AI task\",\n\t\t\"model_endpoint\", h.aiCfg.ModelEndpoint,\n\t\t\"max_tokens\", h.aiCfg.MaxTokens,\n\t\t\"temperature\", h.aiCfg.Temperature,\n\t)\n\n\t// Validate required payload fields\n\ttaskID, ok := evt.Payload[\"task_id\"]\n\tif !ok {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"missing required field: task_id\")\n\t}\n\n\t// Perform AI task (placeholder — real implementation delegates to AI engine)\n\tresult := h.performAITask(ctx, evt.Payload)\n\n\t// Publish completion event\n\tcompleted := events.NewEvent(events.AITaskCompleted, h.cfg.WorkerName, map[string]any{\n\t\t\"task_id\":      taskID,\n\t\t\"status\":       \"completed\",\n\t\t\"result\":       result,\n\t\t\"completed_at\": time.Now().UTC().Format(time.RFC3339),\n\t})\n\tcompleted.WithCorrelationID(evt.CorrelationID)\n\n\tdata, err := completed.Marshal()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"marshal completion event: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tif err := h.prod.Publish(ctx, h.output, data); err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"publish completion event: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tlog.Info(ctx, \"AI task completed\", \"task_id\", taskID)\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// performAITask is a placeholder for the actual AI processing logic.\n// In production, this would call the AI engine service via NATS or HTTP.\nfunc (h *AIHandler) performAITask(ctx context.Context, payload map[string]any) map[string]any {\n\treturn map[string]any{\n\t\t\"summary\":    \"AI task processed\",\n\t\t\"confidence\": 0.95,\n\t}\n}\n","operationIsLocatedInWorkspace":true,"startLineNumbers":[]}

{"tool":"editedExistingFile","path":"modules/graph/handler.go","content":"package main\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/config\"\n\t\"github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/events\"\n\t\"github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/logger\"\n\t\"github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/nats\"\n)\n\n// GraphHandler processes graph intelligence events.\n// It consumes graph.updated events, performs correlation and pattern detection,\n// and publishes graph.enriched events with enriched intelligence.\ntype GraphHandler struct {\n\tcfg    *config.Config\n\tgCfg   *GraphWorkerConfig\n\tlog    *logger.Logger\n\tprod   *nats.Producer\n\toutput string\n}\n\n// Init implements worker.Handler.\nfunc (h *GraphHandler) Init(log *logger.Logger, prod *nats.Producer) {\n\th.log = log\n\th.prod = prod\n}\n\n// Handle processes a graph event.\n// It validates the event, performs graph analysis, and publishes enriched results.\nfunc (h *GraphHandler) Handle(ctx context.Context, evt *events.Event) error {\n\tlog := h.log.WithEvent(evt.ID, evt.Type, evt.CorrelationID)\n\tlog.Info(ctx, \"processing graph event\",\n\t\t\"neo4j_url\", h.gCfg.Neo4jURL,\n\t\t\"max_depth\", h.gCfg.MaxDepth,\n\t)\n\n\t// Validate required payload fields\n\tnodeID, ok := evt.Payload[\"node_id\"]\n\tif !ok {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"missing required field: node_id\")\n\t}\n\n\t// Perform graph analysis (placeholder)\n\tinsights := h.analyzeGraph(ctx, evt.Payload)\n\n\t// Publish enriched graph event\n\tenriched := events.NewEvent(events.GraphEnriched, h.cfg.WorkerName, map[string]any{\n\t\t\"node_id\":     nodeID,\n\t\t\"insights\":    insights,\n\t\t\"analyzed_at\": time.Now().UTC().Format(time.RFC3339),\n\t})\n\tenriched.WithCorrelationID(evt.CorrelationID)\n\n\tdata, err := enriched.Marshal()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"marshal enriched event: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tif err := h.prod.Publish(ctx, h.output, data); err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"publish enriched event: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tlog.Info(ctx, \"graph analysis complete\", \"node_id\", nodeID)\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// analyzeGraph is a placeholder for graph intelligence analysis logic.\nfunc (h *GraphHandler) analyzeGraph(ctx context.Context, payload map[string]any) map[string]any {\n\treturn map[string]any{\n\t\t\"correlations\": []string{},\n\t\t\"patterns\":     []string{},\n\t}\n}\n","operationIsLocatedInWorkspace":true,"startLineNumbers":[]}

{"tool":"editedExistingFile","path":"modules/ioc/handler.go","content":"package main\n\nimport (\n\t\"context\"\n\t\"fmt\"\n\t\"time\"\n\n\t\"github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/config\"\n\t\"github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/events\"\n\t\"github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/logger\"\n\t\"github.com/seclib/Osiris-Lab/pkg/nats\"\n)\n\n// IOCHandler processes IOC ingestion events.\n// It consumes ioc.ingest.requested events, ingests and correlates\n// indicators of compromise, and publishes ioc.ingest.completed events.\ntype IOCHandler struct {\n\tcfg    *config.Config\n\tiocCfg *IOCWorkerConfig\n\tlog    *logger.Logger\n\tprod   *nats.Producer\n\toutput string\n}\n\n// Init implements worker.Handler.\nfunc (h *IOCHandler) Init(log *logger.Logger, prod *nats.Producer) {\n\th.log = log\n\th.prod = prod\n}\n\n// Handle processes an IOC ingestion event.\n// It validates the event, ingests the IOCs, and publishes completion.\nfunc (h *IOCHandler) Handle(ctx context.Context, evt *events.Event) error {\n\tlog := h.log.WithEvent(evt.ID, evt.Type, evt.CorrelationID)\n\tlog.Info(ctx, \"processing IOC ingestion\",\n\t\t\"ioc_source\", h.iocCfg.IOCSource,\n\t\t\"batch_size\", h.iocCfg.BatchSize,\n\t)\n\n\t// Validate required payload fields\n\tsource, ok := evt.Payload[\"source\"]\n\tif !ok {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"missing required field: source\")\n\t}\n\n\t// Ingest IOCs (placeholder)\n\tingested, failed := h.ingestIOCs(ctx, evt.Payload)\n\n\t// Publish completion event\n\tcompleted := events.NewEvent(events.IOCIngestCompleted, h.cfg.WorkerName, map[string]any{\n\t\t\"source\":         source,\n\t\t\"ingested_count\": ingested,\n\t\t\"failed_count\":   failed,\n\t\t\"completed_at\":   time.Now().UTC().Format(time.RFC3339),\n\t})\n\tcompleted.WithCorrelationID(evt.CorrelationID)\n\n\tdata, err := completed.Marshal()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"marshal completion event: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tif err := h.prod.Publish(ctx, h.output, data); err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"publish completion event: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tlog.Info(ctx, \"IOC ingestion complete\", \"source\", source, \"ingested\", ingested, \"failed\", failed)\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// ingestIOCs is a placeholder for IOC ingestion logic.\nfunc (h *IOCHandler) ingestIOCs(ctx context.Context, payload map[string]any) (ingested, failed int) {\n\t// Placeholder: return zero counts\n\treturn 0, 0\n}\n","operationIsLocatedInWorkspace":true,"startLineNumbers":[]}

ThreatHandler struct {\n\tcfg    *config.Config\n\ttCfg   *ThreatWorkerConfig\n\tlog    *logger.Logger\n\tprod   *nats.Producer\n\toutput string\n}\n\n// Init implements worker.Handler.\nfunc (h publishes alerts.\nfunc (h *ThreatHandler) Handle(ctx context.Context, evt *events.Event) error {\n\tlog := h.log.WithEvent(evt.ID, evt.Type, evt.CorrelationID)\n\tlog.Info(ctx, \"processing threat event\",\n\t\t\"alert_threshold\", h.tCfg.AlertThreshold,\n\t\t\"threat_intel_feed\", h.tCfg.ThreatIntelFeed,\n\t)\n\n\t// Validate required payload fields\n\tindicatorID, ok := map[string]any{\n\t\t\t\"indicator_id\": indicatorID,\n\t\t\t\"score\":        score,\n\t\t\t\"severity\":     h.severityFromScore(score),\n\t\t\t\"alerted_at\":   time.Now().UTC().Format(time.RFC3339),\n\t\t})\n\t\talertEvent.WithCorrelationID(evt.CorrelationID)\n\n\t\tdata, err := alertEvent.Marshal()\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"marshal alert event: %w\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif err := h.prod.Publish(ctx, h.output, data); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"publish alert event: %w\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tlog.Info(ctx, \"threat alert published\", \"indicator_id\", indicatorID, \"score\", score)\n\t} else {\n\t\tlog.Info(ctx, \"threat below threshold\", \"indicator_id\", indicatorID, \"score\", score)\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// scoreThreat is a placeholder for threat scoring logic.\nfunc (h *ThreatHandler) scoreThreat(ctx context.Context, payload map[string]any) float64 {\n\t// Placeholder: return a moderate score\n\treturn 0.5\n}\n\n// severityFromScore maps a numeric score to a severity label.\nfunc (h *ThreatHandler) severityFromScore(score float64) string {\n\tswitch {\n\tcase score >= 0.9:\n\t\treturn \"critical\"\n\tcase score >= 0.7:\n\t\treturn \"high\"\n\tcase score >= 0.5:\n\t\treturn \"medium\"\n\tcase score >= 0.3:\n\t\treturn \"low\"\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn {"tool":"editedExistingFile","path":"/","content":"# ============================================================================\n# OSIRIS-Lab v2 — Go Worker Multi-Stage Dockerfile\n# ============================================================================\n# Shared Dockerfile for all Go workers (ai, geoint, graph, ioc, threat).\n# Usage in docker-compose:\n#   build:\n#     context: .\n#     dockerfile: docker/worker.Dockerfile\n#     args:\n#       WORKER_MODULE: modules/ai\n# ============================================================================\n\n# ─── Stage 1: Builder ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nFROM golang:1.24-alpine AS builder\n\nWORKDIR /src\n\n# Copy Go module files first for better layer caching\nCOPY go.mod go.sum ./\nRUN go mod download\n\n# Copy the entire source tree\nCOPY . .\n\n# Build the worker binary\nARG WORKER_MODULE\nRUN CGO_ENABLED=0 go build -o /out/worker ./${WORKER_MODULE}\n\n# ─── Stage 2: Runner ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nFROM alpine:3.20 AS runner\n\n# Security: install ca-certificates for HTTPS\nRUN apk add --no-cache ca-certificates && \\\n    addgroup -S osiris && adduser -S -G osiris osiris\n\n# Copy the binary from the builder stage\nCOPY --from=builder /out/worker /usr/local/bin/worker\n\n# Security: run as non-root\nUSER osiris\n\n# Health check\nHEALTHCHECK --interval=30s --timeout=3s --start-period=5s --retries=3 \\\n    CMD wget -qO- http://localhost:${OSIRIS_HEALTH_PORT:-8080}/health || exit 1\n\nENTRYPOINT [\"worker\"]\n","operationIsLocatedInWorkspace":false,"startLineNumbers":[]}

{"tool":"newFileCreated","path":"docker/worker.Dockerfile","content":"# ============================================================================\n# OSIRIS-Lab v2 — Go Worker Multi-Stage Dockerfile\n# ============================================================================\n# Shared Dockerfile for all Go workers (ai, geoint, graph, ioc, threat).\n# Usage in docker-compose:\n#   build:\n#     context: .\n#     dockerfile: docker/worker.Dockerfile\n#     args:\n#       WORKER_MODULE: modules/ai\n# ============================================================================\n\n# ─── Stage 1: Builder ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nFROM golang:1.24-alpine AS builder\n\nWORKDIR /src\n\n# Copy Go module files first for better layer caching\nCOPY go.mod go.sum ./\nRUN go mod download\n\n# Copy the entire source tree\nCOPY . .\n\n# Build the worker binary\nARG WORKER_MODULE\nRUN CGO_ENABLED=0 go build -o /out/worker ./${WORKER_MODULE}\n\n# ─── Stage 2: Runner ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nFROM alpine:3.20 AS runner\n\n# Security: install ca-certificates for HTTPS\nRUN apk add --no-cache ca-certificates && \\\n    addgroup -S osiris && adduser -S -G osiris osiris\n\n# Copy the binary from the builder stage\nCOPY --from=builder /out/worker /usr/local/bin/worker\n\n# Security: run as non-root\nUSER osiris\n\n# Health check\nHEALTHCHECK --interval=30s --timeout=3s --start-period=5s --retries=3 \\\n    CMD wget -qO- http://localhost:${OSIRIS_HEALTH_PORT:-8080}/health || exit 1\n\nENTRYPOINT [\"worker\"]\n","operationIsLocatedInWorkspace":true,"startLineNumbers":[]}

with open('docker-compose.yml', 'r') as f:
    content = f.read()

# Go worker services to insert before VOLUMES section
workers = '''  # =========================================================================
  # 🧠 AI WORKER — Go (Event Processing)
  # =========================================================================
  ai-worker:
    build:
      context: .
      dockerfile: docker/worker.Dockerfile
      args:
        WORKER_MODULE: modules/ai
    image: osiris-ai-worker:\${GITHUB_SHA:-latest}
    container_name: osiris-ai-worker
    expose:
      - \"8081\"
    environment:
      NATS_URL: nats://nats:4222
      OSIRIS_WORKER_NAME: ai-worker
      OSIRIS_INPUT_SUBJECT: ai.task.created
      OSIRIS_OUTPUT_SUBJECT: ai.task.completed
      OSIRIS_CONSUMER_GROUP: ai-worker
      OSIRIS_STREAM_NAME: AI_TASKS
      OSIRIS_HEALTH_PORT: 8081
      OSIRIS_LOG_LEVEL: info
    restart: unless-stopped
    stop_grace_period: 30s
    security_opt:
      - no-new-privileges:true
    cap_drop:
      - ALL
    read_only: true
    tmpfs:
      - /tmp:size=64M
    deploy:
      resources:
        limits:
          memory: 512M
          cpus: \"0.5\"
        reservations:
          memory: 256M
          cpus: \"0.25\"
    healthcheck:
      test: [\"CMD\", \"wget\", \"--spider\", \"-q\", \"http://localhost:8081/health\"]
      interval: 30s
      timeout: 5s
      start_period: 10s
      retries: 3
    logging:
      driver: \"json-file\"
      options:
        max-size: \"10m\"
        max-file: \"3\"
    networks:
      - osiris-internal-api
      - osiris-event-bus
    depends_on:
      nats:
        condition: service_healthy

  # =========================================================================
  # 🗺️ GEOINT WORKER — Go (Event Processing)
  # =========================================================================
  geoint-worker:
    build:
      context: .
      dockerfile: docker/worker.Dockerfile
      args:
        WORKER_MODULE: modules/geoint
    image: osiris-geoint-worker:\${GITHUB_SHA:-latest}
    container_name: osiris-geoint-worker
    expose:
      - \"8082\"
    environment:
      NATS_URL: nats://nats:4222
      OSIRIS_WORKER_NAME: geoint-worker
      OSIRIS_INPUT_SUBJECT: geoint.processed
      OSIRIS_OUTPUT_SUBJECT: geoint.anomaly
      OSIRIS_CONSUMER_GROUP: geoint-worker
      OSIRIS_STREAM_NAME: GEOINT_EVENTS
      OSIRIS_HEALTH_PORT: 8082
      OSIRIS_LOG_LEVEL: info
    restart: unless-stopped
    stop_grace_period: 30s
    security_opt:
      - no-new-privileges:true
    cap_drop:
      - ALL
    read_only: true
    tmpfs:
      - /tmp:size:64M
    deploy:
      resources:
        limits:
          memory: 512M
          cpus: \"0.5\"
        reservations:
          memory: 256M
          cpus: \"0.25\"
    healthcheck:
      test: [\"CMD\", \"wget\", \"--spider\", \"-q\", \"http://localhost:8082/health\"]
      interval: 30s
      timeout: 5s
      start_period: 10s
      retries: 3
    logging:
      driver: \"json-file\"
      options:
        max-size: \"10m\"
        max-file: \"3\"
    networks:
      - osiris-internal-api
      - osiris-event-bus
    depends_on:
      nats:
        condition: service_healthy

  # =========================================================================
  # 🔗 GRAPH WORKER — Go (Event Processing)
  # =========================================================================
  graph-worker:
    build:
      context: .
      dockerfile: docker/worker.Dockerfile
      args:
        WORKER_MODULE: modules/graph
    image: osiris-graph-worker:\${GITHUB_SHA:-latest}
    container_name: osiris-graph-worker
    expose:
      - \"8083\"
    environment:
      NATS_URL: nats://nats:4222
      OSIRIS_WORKER_NAME: graph-worker
      OSIRIS_INPUT_SUBJECT: graph.updated
      OSIRIS_OUTPUT_SUBJECT: graph.enriched
      OSIRIS_CONSUMER_GROUP: graph-worker
      OSIRIS_STREAM_NAME: GRAPH_EVENTS
      OSIRIS_HEALTH_PORT: 8083
      OSIRIS_LOG_LEVEL: info
    restart: unless-stopped
    stop_grace_period: 30s
    security_opt:
      - no-new-privileges:true
    cap_drop:
      - ALL
    read_only: true
    tmpfs:
      - /tmp:size:64M
    deploy:
      resources:
        limits:
          memory: 512M
          cpus: \"0.5\"
        reservations:
          memory: 256M
          cpus: \"0.25\"
    healthcheck:
      test: [\"CMD\", \"wget\", \"--spider\", \"-q\", \"http://localhost:8083/health\"]
      interval: 30s
      timeout: 5s
      start_period: 10s
      retries: 3
    logging:
      driver: \"json-file\"
      options:
        max-size: \"10m\"
        max-file: \"3\"
    networks:
      - osiris-internal-api
      - osiris-event-bus
    depends_on:
      nats:
        condition: service_healthy

  # =========================================================================
  # 🛡️ IOC WORKER — Go (Event Processing)
  # =========================================================================
  ioc-worker:
    build:
      context: .
      dockerfile: docker/worker.Dockerfile
      args:
        WORKER_MODULE: modules/ioc
    image: osiris-ioc-worker:\${GITHUB_SHA:-latest}
    container_name: osiris-ioc-worker
    expose:
      - \"8085\"
    environment:
      NATS_URL: nats://nats:4222
      OSIRIS_WORKER_NAME: ioc-worker
      OSIRIS_INPUT_SUBJECT: ioc.ingest.requested
      OSIRIS_OUTPUT_SUBJECT: ioc.ingest.completed
      OSIRIS_CONSUMER_GROUP: ioc-worker
      OSIRIS_STREAM_NAME: IOC_EVENTS
      OSIRIS_HEALTH_PORT: 8085
      OSIRIS_LOG_LEVEL: info
    restart: unless-stopped
    stop_grace_period: 30s
    security_opt:
      - no-new-privileges:true
    cap_drop:
      - ALL
    read_only: true
    tmpfs:
      - /tmp:size:64M
    deploy:
      resources:
        limits:
          memory: 512M
          cpus: \"0.5\"
        reservations:
          memory: 256M
          cpus: \"0.25\"
    healthcheck:
      test: [\"CMD\", \"wget\", \"--spider\", \"-q\", \"http://localhost:8085/health\"]
      interval: 30s
      timeout: 5s
      start_period: 10s
      retries: 3
    logging:
      driver: \"json-file\"
      options:
        max-size: \"10m\"
        max-file: \"3\"
    networks:
      - osiris-internal-api
      - osiris-event-bus
    depends_on:
      nats:
        condition: service_healthy

  # =========================================================================
  # ⚠️ THREAT WORKER — Go (Event Processing)
  # =========================================================================
  threat-worker:
    build:
      context: .
      dockerfile: docker/worker.Dockerfile
      args:
        WORKER_MODULE: modules/threat
    image: osiris-threat-worker:\${GITHUB_SHA:-latest}
    container_name: osiris-threat-worker
    expose:
      - \"8084\"
    environment:
      NATS_URL: nats://nats:4222
      OSIRIS_WORKER_NAME: threat-worker
      OSIRIS_INPUT_SUBJECT: threat.analyzed
      OSIRIS_OUTPUT_SUBJECT: threat.alert
      OSIRIS_CONSUMER_GROUP: threat-worker
      OSIRIS_STREAM_NAME: THREAT_EVENTS
      OSIRIS_HEALTH_PORT: 8084
      OSIRIS_LOG_LEVEL: info
    restart: unless-stopped
    stop_grace_period: 30s
    security_opt:
      - no-new-privileges:true
    cap_drop:
      - ALL
    read_only: true
    tmpfs:
      - /tmp:size:64M
    deploy:
      resources:
        limits:
          memory: 512M
          cpus: \"0.5\"
        reservations:
          memory: 256M
          cpus: \"0.25\"
    healthcheck:
      test: [\"CMD\", \"wget\", \"--spider\", \"-q\", \"http://localhost:8084/health\"]
      interval: 30s
      timeout: 5s
      start_period: 10s
      retries: 3
    logging:
      driver: \"json-file\"
      options:
        max-size: \"10m\"
        max-file: \"3\"
    networks:
      - osiris-internal-api
      - osiris-event-bus
    depends_on:
      nats:
        condition: service_healthy

'''

# Insert before the VOLUMES section
content = content.replace('# ─── VOLUMES ─', workers + '# ─── VOLUMES ─')

with open('docker-compose.yml', 'w') as f:
    f.write(content)

print('Go worker services added to docker-compose.yml')
"
